}\n  }\n</style>\n\n<div class=\"page\">\n\n  <div class=\"eyebrow\">Evidence memo &middot; prepared for Sales leadership</div>\n  <h1>Does the CRM carry a lead all the way to a closed deal?</h1>\n  <p class=\"subtitle\">A plain-language answer, backed by a real end-to-end run of the system rather than a description of what it&rsquo;s meant to do.</p>\n  <div class=\"meta-row\">\n    <span><strong>Prepared</strong> 13 Aug 2026</span>\n    <span><strong>Source run</strong> <code style=\"font-family: ui-monospace, monospace;\">scenario run lead-to-won</code></span>\n    <span><strong>Re-checked</strong> automatically on every code change</span>\n  </div>\n\n  <div class=\"verdict\">\n    <p><span class=\"yes\">Yes</span> &mdash; one lead is walked, by the real system, from first capture through qualification, conversion, quoting, a human discount decision, e-signature, an order, and into a signed commercial contract with delivery handed off. Every step below is something the software actually did, not something it claims to do.</p>\n    <p>The one honest caveat: this proves the <em>engine</em> works, end to end, against realistic stand-ins for outside services. It does not yet prove the screens are pleasant to use, that money gets billed and collected, or that it&rsquo;s safe with more than one person&rsquo;s data at a time. Details below.</p>\n  </div>\n\n  <section>\n    <div class=\"section-head\">\n      <div>\n        <div class=\"eyebrow\">The journey, proven step by step</div>\n        <h2>Lead to signed, delivered deal</h2>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n    <p class=\"section-note\">Each row is one thing the system was actually run through &mdash; not a feature list. The evidence tag names the automated check a person can re-run at any time to see it happen again.</p>\n\n    <div class=\"stage-list\">\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">01</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Lead captured</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">A new lead lands in the system in a fresh, untouched state &mdash; the starting line every deal below begins from.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\">3 leads captured and observed &middot; <code>module: lead</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">02</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Qualified or disqualified</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">Qualifying a lead automatically opens exactly one follow-up task for a rep. Disqualifying opens none &mdash; nobody gets busywork chasing a dead end.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>lead.qualify</code> / <code>lead.disqualify</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">03</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Converted to Account, Contacts &amp; Opportunity</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">Qualified leads become one shared company record, two contacts and two open opportunities &mdash; and the system was checked to make sure the same company isn&rsquo;t created twice by accident.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>lead.convert</code> &rarr; 1 company, 2 contacts, 2 opportunities</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">04</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Walked through the pipeline to Won (or Lost)</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">One opportunity is walked, stage by stage, all the way to Won; a second is marked Lost with a reason on file. Once a deal is Won or Lost, the system locks it &mdash; it cannot be quietly reopened.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>opportunity.move-stage</code> &middot; 1 won, 1 lost, terminal stages locked</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">05</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Enriched, scored and routed</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">Separately, three more leads are auto-enriched, scored by a model that shows its reasoning (not a black-box number), and routed to the right owner under a stated, published policy &mdash; with a paper trail of why.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>lead.enrich</code> &rarr; <code>lead.score</code> &rarr; <code>lead.route</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">06</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Quoted from the real catalog</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">A quote is built from actual product and price-book data &mdash; one-time fees, monthly and annual charges, tiered and per-unit pricing &mdash; not a rep typing in a number.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>opportunity.create-quote</code> &rarr; <code>quote.add-line</code> &rarr; <code>quote.submit</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">07</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Discount decided</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">One quote auto-approves because it falls inside policy. The other requires a named human to decide &mdash; with reject, revise, and a clean second version all exercised. The AI can surface a recommendation; it cannot quietly wave a discount through.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>quote.approve</code> / <code>quote.reject</code> / <code>quote.revise</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">08</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Signed and turned into one Order</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">The approved quote goes out for signature. A faked signature callback is caught and refused. A genuine, verified signature produces exactly one order &mdash; never zero, never a duplicate &mdash; and a declined signature creates no order at all.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>quote.request-signature</code> &rarr; verified event &rarr; 1 immutable Order</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">09</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Order activated into a Contract &amp; Subscription</span><span class=\"pill partial\">Proven, one caveat</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">The signed order becomes a real commercial contract with a subscription and a clear list of what&rsquo;s owed to the customer next. One line item the policy genuinely couldn&rsquo;t classify on its own was correctly kicked to a human, with reasons on file, instead of guessing.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>order.activate-contract</code> &rarr; Contract, Contract Version, Subscription &middot; contract term is recorded as after-the-fact metadata, not read off the signed document itself</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">10</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Delivery handed off</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">What the contract obligates the company to deliver is handed to a delivery project with work packages, a subcontracted partner, and a milestone plan &mdash; the baton actually passes, it isn&rsquo;t just noted somewhere.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>commercial-contract.create-delivery-handover</code> &rarr; Delivery Project, work packages, milestones</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n    </div>\n  </section>\n\n  <div class=\"fold-note\">\n    <strong>A subtlety worth knowing:</strong> a rep marking a deal &ldquo;Won&rdquo; (step 4) and the deal actually being signed into an order (step 8) are two separate, both-proven moments tied to the same opportunity &mdash; not one gate. In this run, the deal is marked Won in the pipeline first, and the quote/signature/contract paperwork follows on the same record afterward. That matches how most sales orgs actually work, but it means &ldquo;Won&rdquo; on the board isn&rsquo;t itself proof that paper was ever signed &mdash; both need to be true, and both are checked separately here.\n  </div>\n\n  <section>\n    <div class=\"section-head\">\n      <div>\n        <div class=\"eyebrow\">Read this before repeating the claim upward</div>\n        <h2>What it cannot yet support</h2>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n    <p class=\"section-note\">None of these are secretly broken &mdash; they&rsquo;re acknowledged out loud, on purpose, so nobody discovers them the hard way in front of a customer.</p>\n\n    <div class=\"gap-list\">\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">No billing, invoicing, or revenue recognition</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">The proven story stops at &ldquo;contract signed and delivery started.&rdquo; Getting the customer actually invoiced and the payment recorded is not built.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">The outside connections are realistic stand-ins, not live services</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">Enrichment and e-signature both run against fixed, checked-in fixtures that behave like the real providers. No live third-party service (a real DocuSign, a real data-enrichment vendor) has been wired in and proven yet.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">No logins, no per-person access, no separation between customers&rsquo; data</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">Today this only makes sense as a single-operator, local system. It is not yet safe to hand to a team, let alone run multiple customers&rsquo; data through it.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">Nothing runs on a clock by itself</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">No automatic reminders, SLA countdowns, renewal nudges, or scheduled follow-ups. Every next step happens because a person (or another system) explicitly triggers it.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">Nobody has actually clicked through the screens</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">This evidence proves the underlying engine does the right thing. It is not proof that a rep using the actual on-screen forms has a smooth experience &mdash; that check is still manual and hasn&rsquo;t been automated.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n    </div>\n  </section>\n\n  <footer>\n    This is not a description of intended behavior &mdash; it is the reported result of running <code style=\"font-family: ui-monospace, monospace;\">scenario run lead-to-won</code>, a checked-in, deterministic, offline test that drives the real system and is required to pass before any change to the codebase ships. It can be re-run at any time and will report the same result on an unchanged system. Out of roughly 150 tracked business capabilities in the wider product, this one run establishes 15 directly; the rest are either proven by other, separate runs (support-case handling, for instance) or are the honestly-labeled roadmap items above &mdash; &ldquo;not proven by this run&rdquo; is never the same claim as &ldquo;doesn&rsquo;t work.&rdquo;\n  </footer>\n\n</div>\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4788,"cache_read_input_tokens":82573,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4788},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"b4fdfc33-27db-4d2a-8188-c2fb317daf9a","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:39:32.231Z","request_id":"req_011Ce1dLnY3xMd7MZbEqjtbK"}

}\n  }\n</style>\n\n<div class=\"page\">\n\n  <div class=\"eyebrow\">Evidence memo &middot; prepared for Sales leadership</div>\n  <h1>Does the CRM carry a lead all the way to a closed deal?</h1>\n  <p class=\"subtitle\">A plain-language answer, backed by a real end-to-end run of the system rather than a description of what it&rsquo;s meant to do.</p>\n  <div class=\"meta-row\">\n    <span><strong>Prepared</strong> 13 Aug 2026</span>\n    <span><strong>Source run</strong> <code style=\"font-family: ui-monospace, monospace;\">scenario run lead-to-won</code></span>\n    <span><strong>Re-checked</strong> automatically on every code change</span>\n  </div>\n\n  <div class=\"verdict\">\n    <p><span class=\"yes\">Yes</span> &mdash; one lead is walked, by the real system, from first capture through qualification, conversion, quoting, a human discount decision, e-signature, an order, and into a signed commercial contract with delivery handed off. Every step below is something the software actually did, not something it claims to do.</p>\n    <p>The one honest caveat: this proves the <em>engine</em> works, end to end, against realistic stand-ins for outside services. It does not yet prove the screens are pleasant to use, that money gets billed and collected, or that it&rsquo;s safe with more than one person&rsquo;s data at a time. Details below.</p>\n  </div>\n\n  <section>\n    <div class=\"section-head\">\n      <div>\n        <div class=\"eyebrow\">The journey, proven step by step</div>\n        <h2>Lead to signed, delivered deal</h2>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n    <p class=\"section-note\">Each row is one thing the system was actually run through &mdash; not a feature list. The evidence tag names the automated check a person can re-run at any time to see it happen again.</p>\n\n    <div class=\"stage-list\">\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">01</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Lead captured</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">A new lead lands in the system in a fresh, untouched state &mdash; the starting line every deal below begins from.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\">3 leads captured and observed &middot; <code>module: lead</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">02</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Qualified or disqualified</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">Qualifying a lead automatically opens exactly one follow-up task for a rep. Disqualifying opens none &mdash; nobody gets busywork chasing a dead end.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>lead.qualify</code> / <code>lead.disqualify</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">03</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Converted to Account, Contacts &amp; Opportunity</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">Qualified leads become one shared company record, two contacts and two open opportunities &mdash; and the system was checked to make sure the same company isn&rsquo;t created twice by accident.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>lead.convert</code> &rarr; 1 company, 2 contacts, 2 opportunities</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">04</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Walked through the pipeline to Won (or Lost)</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">One opportunity is walked, stage by stage, all the way to Won; a second is marked Lost with a reason on file. Once a deal is Won or Lost, the system locks it &mdash; it cannot be quietly reopened.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>opportunity.move-stage</code> &middot; 1 won, 1 lost, terminal stages locked</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">05</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Enriched, scored and routed</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">Separately, three more leads are auto-enriched, scored by a model that shows its reasoning (not a black-box number), and routed to the right owner under a stated, published policy &mdash; with a paper trail of why.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>lead.enrich</code> &rarr; <code>lead.score</code> &rarr; <code>lead.route</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">06</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Quoted from the real catalog</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">A quote is built from actual product and price-book data &mdash; one-time fees, monthly and annual charges, tiered and per-unit pricing &mdash; not a rep typing in a number.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>opportunity.create-quote</code> &rarr; <code>quote.add-line</code> &rarr; <code>quote.submit</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">07</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Discount decided</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">One quote auto-approves because it falls inside policy. The other requires a named human to decide &mdash; with reject, revise, and a clean second version all exercised. The AI can surface a recommendation; it cannot quietly wave a discount through.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>quote.approve</code> / <code>quote.reject</code> / <code>quote.revise</code></div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">08</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Signed and turned into one Order</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">The approved quote goes out for signature. A faked signature callback is caught and refused. A genuine, verified signature produces exactly one order &mdash; never zero, never a duplicate &mdash; and a declined signature creates no order at all.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>quote.request-signature</code> &rarr; verified event &rarr; 1 immutable Order</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">09</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Order activated into a Contract &amp; Subscription</span><span class=\"pill partial\">Proven, one caveat</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">The signed order becomes a real commercial contract with a subscription and a clear list of what&rsquo;s owed to the customer next. One line item the policy genuinely couldn&rsquo;t classify on its own was correctly kicked to a human, with reasons on file, instead of guessing.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>order.activate-contract</code> &rarr; Contract, Contract Version, Subscription &middot; contract term is recorded as after-the-fact metadata, not read off the signed document itself</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"stage\">\n        <div class=\"stage-num\">10</div>\n        <div class=\"stage-body\">\n          <div class=\"stage-top\"><span class=\"stage-name\">Delivery handed off</span><span class=\"pill proven\">Proven</span></div>\n          <p class=\"stage-desc\">What the contract obligates the company to deliver is handed to a delivery project with work packages, a subcontracted partner, and a milestone plan &mdash; the baton actually passes, it isn&rsquo;t just noted somewhere.</p>\n          <div class=\"stage-evidence\"><code>commercial-contract.create-delivery-handover</code> &rarr; Delivery Project, work packages, milestones</div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n    </div>\n  </section>\n\n  <div class=\"fold-note\">\n    <strong>A subtlety worth knowing:</strong> a rep marking a deal &ldquo;Won&rdquo; (step 4) and the deal actually being signed into an order (step 8) are two separate, both-proven moments tied to the same opportunity &mdash; not one gate. In this run, the deal is marked Won in the pipeline first, and the quote/signature/contract paperwork follows on the same record afterward. That matches how most sales orgs actually work, but it means &ldquo;Won&rdquo; on the board isn&rsquo;t itself proof that paper was ever signed &mdash; both need to be true, and both are checked separately here.\n  </div>\n\n  <section>\n    <div class=\"section-head\">\n      <div>\n        <div class=\"eyebrow\">Read this before repeating the claim upward</div>\n        <h2>What it cannot yet support</h2>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n    <p class=\"section-note\">None of these are secretly broken &mdash; they&rsquo;re acknowledged out loud, on purpose, so nobody discovers them the hard way in front of a customer.</p>\n\n    <div class=\"gap-list\">\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">No billing, invoicing, or revenue recognition</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">The proven story stops at &ldquo;contract signed and delivery started.&rdquo; Getting the customer actually invoiced and the payment recorded is not built.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">The outside connections are realistic stand-ins, not live services</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">Enrichment and e-signature both run against fixed, checked-in fixtures that behave like the real providers. No live third-party service (a real DocuSign, a real data-enrichment vendor) has been wired in and proven yet.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">No logins, no per-person access, no separation between customers&rsquo; data</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">Today this only makes sense as a single-operator, local system. It is not yet safe to hand to a team, let alone run multiple customers&rsquo; data through it.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">Nothing runs on a clock by itself</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">No automatic reminders, SLA countdowns, renewal nudges, or scheduled follow-ups. Every next step happens because a person (or another system) explicitly triggers it.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div class=\"gap-item\">\n        <div class=\"gap-mark\">&mdash;</div>\n        <div>\n          <div class=\"gap-title\">Nobody has actually clicked through the screens</div>\n          <p class=\"gap-desc\">This evidence proves the underlying engine does the right thing. It is not proof that a rep using the actual on-screen forms has a smooth experience &mdash; that check is still manual and hasn&rsquo;t been automated.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n    </div>\n  </section>\n\n  <footer>\n    This is not a description of intended behavior &mdash; it is the reported result of running <code style=\"font-family: ui-monospace, monospace;\">scenario run lead-to-won</code>, a checked-in, deterministic, offline test that drives the real system and is required to pass before any change to the codebase ships. It can be re-run at any time and will report the same result on an unchanged system. Out of roughly 150 tracked business capabilities in the wider product, this one run establishes 15 directly; the rest are either proven by other, separate runs (support-case handling, for instance) or are the honestly-labeled roadmap items above &mdash; &ldquo;not proven by this run&rdquo; is never the same claim as &ldquo;doesn&rsquo;t work.&rdquo;\n  </footer>\n\n</div>\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
